● Commit a34f63e: "synced with work pc, add jev server support, need to clean it
  up tho"

  It's a large sync commit: 63 files, +5319/−1998. It covers five separate
  areas.

  1. JEV: server backend and per-project selection

  - New jev/backends/llm2jev.py: a backend for llm2jev's POST /v1/systemone,
    which sits in front of sglang/vllm. It sends one HTTP call per state with at
    most 64 questions, runs up to 20 concurrent calls
    (WIKI_JEV_LLM2JEV_CONCURRENCY), handles noul/choice/score questions, and
    retries once on transport errors or 5xx. The aliases systemone, sglang, vllm
    and jpt all resolve to it. About 150 lines of tests were added in
    jev/test_jev.py.
  - get_engine_for(settings) and JevConfig.from_settings(): a project's INI can
    now choose the JEV backend and its GGUF paths, and that choice overrides
    .env. Every linker and index call site now uses this instead of
    get_engine().
  - projectC.ini now runs neo linking with JEV on a local GGUF model: Q4_K_M,
    batched mode, tiebreak off.
  - main.py check now also checks JEV. For gguf it confirms the model files and
    the jev-score binary exist, then runs one real decision.

  2. Linker

  - jev_judge.py: the target is encoded once per stage and each candidate is
    sent as a question against it, instead of repeating the full state for every
    candidate. Long candidate bodies are split into parts of about 1200 tokens,
    and the candidate keeps its best score. Previously bodies were cut at 6000
    characters.
  - Team is now per project (Path(project.root).name) rather than the first
    folder of the mount path. This affects the catalog, the service and the
    related-docs index.
  - Link budgets are larger and configurable through settings:

  | Setting                            | Before   | After    |
  |------------------------------------|----------|----------|
  | screen candidates                  | 50       | 80       |
  | verify top                         | 5        | 20       |
  | curate keep                        | 8        | 12       |
  | hop caps                           | 40,40,20 | 60,50,25 |
  | footer links                       | 15       | 20       |
  | edge candidates / edges per target | 8 / 2    | 12 / 3   |
    Inline and footer caps now come through render_limits().
  - Incremental fix: patched chunks (not just regenerated ones) now get fresh
    metadata and embeddings.

  3. Publisher, queue and diff pipeline

  - --continue on sync, watch and queue work: candidate worktrees are now kept
    when a run exits (keep=True), and a later run with --continue picks up an
    interrupted building/prepared transaction with its LLM checkpoints intact.
    It can also adopt an orphaned worktree whose transaction row was lost. Runs
    without the flag discard leftover worktrees at startup.
  - Resume without re-parsing: failed ledger rows now keep source_sha256, so a
    retry of an unchanged source reuses the raw Markdown instead of calling the
    parser again. An interrupted first wiki build resumes instead of wiping its
    state (_wiki_run_complete).
  - Index upkeep: after each promoted batch, the local metadata/index/** files
    are rebuilt from live state. Moves refresh both the old and new trees. A
    full sync also deletes stale local and GROWI index pages.
  - Collision guards: assert_unique_generated_paths and
    _assert_unique_growi_locations stop mount paths that would map to the same
    raw, wiki or GROWI path.
  - Empty generated directories are cleaned up after deletes, moves and
    promotions.
  - New tests: test_mount_diff_pipeline.py and test_update_tiers.py.

  4. Wiki generation

  - New graph/common/images.py: one parser for every image spelling
    (<image-unit>, <img>, <embed>, Markdown images), keyed by the SHA-256 of the
    decoded bytes. The GROWI client, the incremental diff, chunking and the
    atomic-block checks all use it now. As a result, a change in how an image is
    written but not in its bytes is treated as a no-op (tier 0,
    image-representation).
  - Default temperature is now 0.7 everywhere instead of 0.0, including make_llm
    in graph/clients/chat.py, WikiConfig and wiki_config. Retries also use 0.7
    instead of 1.0.
  - Retry loops with validation feedback were added to seed-plan compile,
    reference research and PPTX section judging. Seed-plan compile no longer
    resumes attempts from an earlier run, and map_attempts dropped from 100
    to 5.
  - Seed-plan compile now merges pages automatically when no safe cut exists
    around an atomic block, instead of failing.

  5. growi-search (the largest part: researcher.py, +1375 lines)

  - JEV-scoped research was reworked: per-project 00-目次 scans, a structured
    intent pass, and a shortlist of the top 20 documents using BM25, embeddings
    and a reranker. That shortlist falls back to scanning everything whenever it
    isn't sure.
  - Compile routing by number of accepted seeds:
    - under 10: one final compiler reads the full page bodies
    - 10 to 32: subagents per document, then the final compiler
    - over 32: a second JEV scan and two-level compilation
  - Compiler inputs are now split into complete chunks against a context budget
    instead of being cut. Output caps are 16k for subagent reports, 32k for the
    level-1 compiler and 64k for the final compiler.
  - The frontend gained 目次-scan status strings and a rewritten Markdown export
    and GROWI viewer helpers.
  - handoff-growi-search.md records the ten bugs fixed in that session and the
    rules you set for it.

  Docs

  - Added: handoff-growi-search.md and codex-projc.md.
  - Deleted: jev_plan.md, jev_report.md and handoff.md.

  Things to check when you clean it up

  - GROWI tokens are committed in plain text: projectB_test.ini got a new token
    in this commit, and projectC.ini already contains the same one. They're in
    git history either way.
  - writer.py:233 forces at least 5 attempts: attempts = max(5, ...) means the
    setting can raise the count but never lower it, while wiki_config still
    defaults write_attempts to 3. It looks like max was meant to be min, or the
    5 was supposed to be the default.
  - handoff-growi-search.md says some work was uncommitted, including the
    narrow-query exact-term check (its bug #10), which it lists as "still
    unverified". That work is probably what landed in this commit's
    researcher.py, so it may still need a test run.
  - legacy.py structured_ainvoke: the first structured call now passes
    temperature and max_tokens to with_structured_output directly, so call_llm
    only serves the fallback path. I checked that the installed langchain_openai
    accepts these arguments, so it works as written.

● Let me read the core flow: ask, _run_jev_sweep, and the 目次 stages.

  Searched for 1 pattern, read 1 file, ran 9 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Short version: the JEV stages sit in front of retrieval and run on the wrong
  units. I'd flip that. Retrieve first on sections of the pages, and use JEV at
  the end, as a short yes/no gate that decides when to stop.

  Why it's slow

  Before any page body is read, one question currently goes through this chain:

  1. A full tree walk that calls the GROWI API live every time and skips the
     mirror (researcher.py:1697, live_list=True).
  2. An LLM intent call (:2021).
  3. JEV over every document 目次, in chunks of up to 15k tokens (:2411).
  4. Five LLM note calls (:2448).
  5. An LLM rewrite (:2450).
  6. A BM25 + embedding + reranker shortlist that re-embeds every 目次 on every
     question, with no cache (:1773).
  7. An LLM coverage check that answers 十分/不足 (:2455).
  8. JEV scoring of every card.

  That is 4 LLM round trips in sequence plus a long-context JEV pass, all before
  the first page is read. Past 32 seeds it then runs a second full sweep plus
  the L1/L2 report merges.

  Why it's inaccurate

  - The unit is too coarse. Everything (keyword overlap, embeddings, the
    reranker, JEV) looks only at the page card: title, one summary line, 12
    keywords and 8 entities. Pages are admitted from the card alone (:2214). So
    bufsize/opentype never reach any index, and "3rd parameter of mpf_mfs_open"
    cannot be routed from the index.
  - JEV is used where it is weakest. A 0.8B classifier reads a 15k-token 目次
    and answers a long question that an LLM rewrote. It does well on a short
    text plus a short, fixed question, and badly on this.
  - The query drifts. It goes raw → intent → rewrite, and the card judge only
    ever sees the rewrite.
  - The shortlist's dense and reranker channels embed and score each 目次 as one
    text. Both models only read up to their maximum input length, so the tail
    of a large 目次 is invisible to them.
  - The mode is picked from the seed count, after the expensive work is done. It
    should come from the question up front.

  Proposal: section index + escalation ladder

  Index, built in the background from the mirror and keyed by page revision:
  - One unit per heading section of every page reachable from a project root
    目次, plus the existing 目次 card as a page-level unit.
  - md.split_sections (markdown.py:107) already splits oversized sections on
    paragraph boundaries without dropping text, so the no-truncation rule holds.
  - Each unit gets three entries:
    - BM25 postings, reusing the term function in _jev_retrieve_tocs
    - a ruri embedding, cached by text hash the way IndexMap._vector_cache does
    - an exact-symbol index of identifiers such as mpf_mfs_open
  - numpy is already a dependency. This gives full retrieval coverage without 
    waiting for the build-time 目次 enrichment; that work becomes a
    nice-to-have.

  Query time. Nothing waits on an LLM before retrieval:

  question ─┬─ JEV shape check (list? / single fact?)     ← cascade.py:_profile 
  already does this
            ├─ symbol lookup (regex → exact postings)
            └─ BM25 + dense + symbol → RRF → top ~100 sections → reranker
                           │
       ┌───────────────────┼──────────────────────────┐
     FACT               NEEDLE                        LIST
   JEV on top 10    JEV along the ranked list      find which documents are in 
  scope, then
   p ≥ 0.8 → read   in batches of 64, stop at      JEV on EVERY section of those
  documents
   → 1 LLM answer   the first confident batch;     → one extraction call per 
  document, in parallel
   else escalate ─→ if the list runs out,          → merge items in Python (no 
  loss)
                    JEV the whole corpus           → 1 final answer call

  Each component does one job:
  - BM25, dense and symbol search find candidates cheaply (recall).
  - The reranker decides what to look at first (ordering).
  - JEV decides whether a short section actually answers the question. Its
    calibrated yes probability is what allows an early exit. Its question stays
    short and fixed, and always uses the raw query.
  - The LLM only reads and writes: extracting and answering.
  - Cross-language case (an English question over the Japanese wiki): one LLM
    translation call, run in parallel as an extra retrieval query.

  LLM calls in the new design:

  ┌────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │ Shape  │                           LLM calls                           │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Fact   │ 1 answer (+ the existing _verified)                           │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ List   │ one extraction per in-scope document, in parallel, + 1 answer │
  ├────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Needle │ 1 answer once found                                           │
  └────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The Python merge in the list path also resolves open item #2 (completeness for
  broad questions): completeness means every section in scope was scored, which
  can be stated precisely rather than inferred from a page count.

  Removed:
  - the intent/notes/rewrite/coverage chain
  - the 15k-token 目次 JEV scan
  - card-only admission
  - the <10 / 10–32 / >32 seed-count thresholds
  - the second sweep
  - the L1/L2 report merges
  - the subagents that explore on their own, except possibly for multi-step
    "why/how" questions

  Kept: the mirror, IndexMap (which pages are reachable, card units, entity
  definers), JEV request batching, the embedder and reranker clients, _verified,
  and the existing SSE events (jev_progress, jev_gate, candidates, compiling).

  Build order

  1. Section index from the mirror.
  2. Hybrid retrieval + reranker.
  3. The fact path with the JEV gate. Test case: 3rd parameter of mpf_mfs_open.
  4. The list path. Test case: "all functions of moove library".
  5. Wire it in as the WIKI_JEV_MODE option that cascade currently occupies, and
     A/B it against exhaustive with the existing eval_run.py. Delete the old
     chain once it wins.

  If you want relief before any of that, two fixes are cheap on their own: build
  the 目次 list from the mirror or IndexMap instead of the live walk, and cache
  the 目次 embeddings.

  Decisions for you

  1. Where the index comes from. I recommend the mirror, which keeps
     growi-search self-contained and matches what is actually on GROWI,
     including human edits. The alternative is the linker's sqlite catalog: it
     has richer per-chunk entities and summaries, but it ties the deployment to
     the build machine and ignores GROWI edits.
  2. Keep the explorer subagents at all? Only for multi-step questions, or drop
     them entirely?

  Tell me which way on both and I'll start with steps 1–3, since the fact path
  is the easiest to measure.

● Today: what happens for every question

  question
     │
     ▼
  walk the whole GROWI tree live            (slow: many API calls, mirror 
  ignored)
     │
     ▼
  LLM: "what does the user mean?"           (LLM call 1)
     │
     ▼
  JEV reads every document's 00-目次        (slow: huge inputs for a tiny model)
     │
     ▼
  LLM: summarise the best 目次s             (LLM call 2)
     │
     ▼
  LLM: rewrite the question                 (LLM call 3, question starts 
  drifting)
     │
     ▼
  LLM: "is this shortlist enough?"          (LLM call 4)
     │
     ▼
  JEV judges each page card                 (card = title + 1 line + keywords)
     │
     ▼
  ... only NOW is a real page read
     │
     ▼
  subagents → merge → merge → answer → verify

  Why it's slow: four LLM calls and two big JEV passes happen one after another
  before any page is opened.

  Why it's wrong: JEV only ever sees a one-line summary of each page. If the
  answer is deep inside a page (for example the parameters of mpf_mfs_open), the
  summary doesn't mention it, so the page gets rejected.

  Proposed: search first, judge last

  Once, in the background, whenever a page changes:

  mirror (all page bodies are already on disk)
     │
     ▼
  cut every page into sections (by heading)
     │
     ▼
  for each section store:  keywords  +  embedding  +  exact names (mpf_mfs_open,
  ...)

  For each question:

  question
     │
     ▼
  JEV: "list question or single-fact question?"   (tiny, fast)
     │
     ▼
  search the sections: keywords + embedding + exact names
     │
     ▼
  reranker sorts the best ~100 sections
     │
     ▼
  pick one of 3 paths ↓

  The 3 paths

  1. Small detail ("3rd parameter of mpf_mfs_open")

  top 10 sections → JEV: "does this answer it?" → yes → LLM writes answer → DONE
  (early exit)
                                                 → no  → go to path 2

  2. Small detail that could be anywhere (the needle hunt)

  walk down the sorted list 64 sections at a time → JEV yes/no
        → found → LLM writes answer → DONE
        → list ran out → JEV checks every section in the wiki → answer

  3. Give me everything ("all functions of moove library")

  find which documents are involved
     → JEV checks EVERY section of those documents (no early exit)
     → LLM pulls the items out of each document (in parallel)
     → Python merges the items (nothing lost)
     → LLM writes the final list

  Who does what

  keywords + embedding  →  find candidates        (cheap, wide net)
  reranker              →  sort them              (best first)
  JEV                   →  yes/no: answers it?    (decides when to stop)
  LLM                   →  read + write answer    (only at the end)

  Before vs after

                          TODAY                        PROPOSED
  LLM calls before
  reading a page          4                            0
  JEV input size          a whole 目次 per document    one short section
  unit searched           1-line page summary          every section of every 
  page
  small-detail question   full pipeline every time     stops after ~10 sections
  list question           2 sweeps + merge chain       one sweep of the relevant
  docs + Python merge

  The two open decisions from before still stand: whether to build the section
  index from the mirror (my pick) or from the linker database, and whether to
  keep the subagents at all.

● 1. One loop instead of 3 paths

  There are no fixed paths. There is one loop, and it follows one rule:

  nothing found yet          → keep looking, and search wider if needed
  found things               → keep looking while each new batch still finds 
  more
  nothing new for a while    → stop looking

  How long "a while" lasts is a dial, not a switch. Before the search starts,
  JEV reads only the question and returns a list-ness score between 0 and 1. A
  score of 0.1 means stop after 1 empty batch; 0.9 means keep going through
  about 4 empty batches. Every value in between works too, so any question lands
  somewhere on that range.

  question
    │
    ▼
  JEV reads the question only → list-ness 0.0 … 1.0            (the dial)
    │
    ▼
  keyword + embedding + exact-name search → reranker → sections, best first
    │
    ▼
  ┌─► take the next 32 sections
  │     │
  │     ▼
  │   JEV asks 2 questions about each section (it reads the section once):
  │     A: "does this section directly answer the question?"
  │     B: "does this section help answer it?"
  │     │
  │     ▼
  │   a hit in document X? → move X's neighbouring sections to the front
  │     │                    (lists usually sit together in one chapter)
  │     ▼
  └── still finding new helpful sections?  (how long to wait = the dial)
        │ no
        ▼
     decide how much reading and thinking is needed          (see §2)

  2. How much thinking comes from what was found, not from how the question 
  looks

  This is what handles your file deadlock case:

  strong A, little else                 → no researchers, 1 LLM call answers
  weak A, lots of B spread over docs    → researchers  (more evidence → more 
  researchers)
  lots of A and B (a list)              → many researchers
  all reports fit in one LLM input?     → 1-stage compiler, otherwise 2-stage

  Here is how "what is file deadlock" would go:

  "what is file deadlock"
    → list-ness 0.1 (looks like a quick question)
    → batch 1: best A = 0.3, because no page defines it
               B hits: 9 sections in 4 docs (lock functions, exclusive open, 
  error codes)
    → batch 2: 5 more B hits → keep going.  batch 3: nothing new → stop
    → weak A + widespread B = "this needs reasoning"
    → A is weak, so one LLM call lists related terms (排他, ロック, 
  同時オープン)
      and one more search round runs on those terms
    → 3 researchers, each given one cluster of sections plus read/search/follow 
  tools
    → the reports are small → 1-stage compiler → answer

  These examples show where different questions would land on the range. The
  numbers are made up for illustration, not measured:

  question                       list-ness  best A  B spread        researchers 
  compiler
  3rd param of mpf_mfs_open      0.05       0.95    1 page          0           
  none (1 LLM)
  what is file deadlock          0.1        0.3     4 docs          3           
  1-stage
  which funcs return EBUSY       0.5        0.8     many pages      2-4         
  1-stage
  all functions of moove         0.95       high    whole chapter   10+         
  2-stage

  3. Researchers and compiler: kept, with better input

  - Researchers get clusters of sections instead of bare page IDs. They keep the
    same tools (read, follow_link, find), and search now queries the new
    section index. The existing _run_seed_groups, _run_group_subagent,
    _fold_reports and _synthesize_reports get reused.
  - The switch to a 2-stage compiler depends on size. If all the reports fit in
    one LLM input, one stage is enough; otherwise it runs 2 stages. Today it's
    decided by counting seeds. This is the rule you already agreed to (open item
    #6).
  - Optional extra round. The compiler can return a list of gaps, which triggers
    exactly one more round of searching and researchers, never more.

  4. Live sync with GROWI

  The mirror already does most of this work. Only the section indexer at the
  bottom is new.

  GROWI
    │  mirror polls every 10 s (audit log, or recent pages + a full relist every
  30 min)   ← exists
    ▼
  mirror on disk: every page body, stored by page id + revision                 
            ← exists
    │  mirror.version changes
    ▼
  section indexer (new background thread)
    │  compares its own {page id → revision} list with the mirror's
    ▼
  changed pages only: split by heading → hash each section's text
    │
    ├─ hash already known → reuse the embedding   (renames and moves cost 
  nothing)
    └─ new hash           → embed it (ruri, one batch at a time; the LLM/embed 
  server is shared)
    │
    ▼
  deleted pages → their sections are dropped

  The index is exactly as fresh as the mirror:

  - Edits: show up within seconds.
  - Deletes: show up immediately in audit-log mode, or within 30 minutes in
    recent-pages mode.
  - First run: the only slow step is the one-time full embedding, which runs in
    the background.

  5. Where everything is stored

  growi-search is deliberately database-free: tests/test_researcher.py:1050
  fails the build if anything imports sqlite3. So the index is plain files
  inside the mirror folder:

  data/growi-search-mirror/<ns>/
    pages/              page bodies                         (exists)
    catalog.json.gz     page list + revisions               (exists)
    sections/
      vectors.npy       one row per section text, float16   ← the only expensive
  part, kept on disk
      hashes.json       which text hash each row belongs to

  These live in memory and are rebuilt from the mirror at startup, which is
  cheap:

  - the section table (page, heading, line range, hash)
  - keyword postings (term → sections)
  - section keywords

  Size: each section takes 768 × 2 bytes, so 50k sections is about 77 MB.
  Searching that with numpy (already installed) takes milliseconds.

  6. Section keywords without an LLM

  section text
    │
    ├─ exact names      regex: mpf_mfs_open, EBUSY, MPF_MFS_READLOCK
    ├─ word runs        regex: katakana runs (デッドロック), kanji runs 
  (排他制御), latin words
    ├─ markup           headings, `code`, **bold**, table header cells
    └─ known entities   from the 目次 cards, found with the existing 
  _page_entities matcher
    │
    ▼
  score by TF-IDF across all sections (rare in the wiki + frequent in this 
  section = keyword)
    │
    ▼
  keep the top ~20 per section

  This takes milliseconds per section and makes no network calls. No Japanese
  tokenizer is installed (I checked for sudachi, mecab, janome and fugashi), and
  none is needed: IndexMap.grams already gets by the same way.

  A missing keyword can never hide a section, because search reads the full text
  (character pairs plus embeddings). Keywords are only used for three things:

  - exact-name matching
  - short previews for JEV and the researchers
  - a 目次-style display

  Next step

  1. Build the section indexer on top of the mirror.
  2. Build the search loop.

❯ its not just about "listness" this is not fact retreival, what if the user    
  wants to understand a mechanism explained? it needs subagents running across  
  seeds to understand what is happening                                         

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● You're right. Reducing everything to "list-ness" was the flaw in my last
  answer. A question like "how does X happen" isn't about how many items to
  find. The answer isn't written down anywhere, so someone has to read several
  pieces and connect them.

  Replace the single dial with two measured dials

  dial 1  WHERE     how many places hold relevant material?    → how many 
  researchers per round
  dial 2  HOW FAR   is the answer written down, or must it     → how many rounds
  of research
                    be pieced together from several parts?

  - Fact lookup: 1 place, written down. No researchers.
  - List: many places, but each item is written down. One wide round.
  - Mechanism ("what is file deadlock"): a few places, and nothing states the
    answer. Several narrow rounds that connect the pieces.

  Neither dial is guessed from how the question is worded; both are measured.
  Dial 1 comes from the search results. Dial 2 comes from the lead checking,
  after each round, whether the explanation is complete yet.

  Why today's subagents struggle with mechanism questions

  seeds split by DOCUMENT           → lock functions in doc A, open modes in doc
  B
  each subagent is BLOCKED from       (researcher.py:3162  blocked = everyone - 
  owned[pos])
  reading other groups' seeds       → no single subagent sees both sides of the 
  interaction
  prompt asks "what does THIS       → the reports cover one document each, not 
  cause and effect
  document say" (:3279)
  ONE round only                    → nobody asks "what is still unexplained?"
  no "who defines X?" tool          → IndexMap.entity_definers exists, but 
  subagents can't use it

  New flow

  question
    │
    ▼
  FIND  (fast, no LLM)
    section search + reranker + JEV per section:
      A = "does this section answer it directly?"
      B = "does this section help?"
    → evidence map: the helpful sections, the names in them, and the links 
  between them
    │
    ▼
  QUICK EXIT only if: A is high (written in one place) AND the question doesn't 
  ask how or why
    │ otherwise
    ▼
  GROUP the evidence into THREADS by connection (shared function names / links),
  not by document
    │
    ▼
  ROUND 1: one researcher per thread
    - may read ANY seed (no blocking; the shared page cache already avoids 
  repeat downloads)
    - tools: read, follow_link, search (the section index), definition(name)
    - report = what I found  +  what I still don't understand
    │
    ▼
  LEAD CHECK (1 LLM call over the reports)
    "is the explanation complete? which questions are still open?"
    ├─ gaps  → each gap becomes a round-2 question,
    │          seeded with the sections from BOTH sides of it + a fresh search 
  on the gap
    │          → ROUND 2 → LEAD CHECK → ...          (hard cap, e.g. 3 rounds)
    └─ complete
    │
    ▼
  COMPILER: 1 stage if all reports fit in one LLM input, otherwise 2 stages

  "what is file deadlock", step by step

  This is an illustration built from your description. It doesn't quote your
  docs.

  FIND      no section defines it           → A max ≈ 0.3
            helpful sections in 3 areas     → lock functions, file open modes, 
  error/wait behaviour
  QUICK?    no (A is low)
  GROUP     3 threads (they share lock / file-handle names)
  ROUND 1   r1: "function type A takes the file lock and holds it"
            r2: "function type B waits for that lock"
            r3: "a certain error appears when the wait never ends"
            open question: "what if A and B run at the same time?"
  LEAD      gap found → round 2
  ROUND 2   1 researcher, seeded with r1's + r2's sections
            → "A and B running together each wait on the other: that is the file
  deadlock"
  LEAD      complete
  COMPILER  reports are small → 1 stage → answer

  The question looked like a quick lookup, but no section answered it directly
  (A stayed low). That alone rules out the quick exit, and the lead's gap check
  is what triggered the second, deeper round.

  Other shapes

  3rd param of mpf_mfs_open    FIND: A=0.95, 1 page         → quick exit, 1 LLM 
  call
  all functions of moove       FIND: 12 threads             → 1 wide round, no 
  gaps → 2-stage compiler
  what is file deadlock        FIND: A low, 3 threads       → 2 rounds, 1-stage 
  compiler
  why does X fail after Y      FIND: A low, many threads    → wide + several 
  rounds → 2-stage

  Code changes on top of the section index

  1. _seed_groups: group by shared names and links instead of by document.
  2. Remove the offlimits read blocking. Duplicate work is already cheap because
     of the shared cache, and each researcher is told which threads the others
     own.
  3. Subagent report format: findings + open questions.
  4. New definition(name) tool, backed by IndexMap.definers_for.
  5. Point the subagent search tool at the section index instead of GROWI's
     Elasticsearch.
  6. Add the lead gap-check loop around _run_seed_groups.
  7. Choose 1 vs 2 compiler stages by input size, not by seed count.

  Everything else stays as described last time: the mirror-based section index,
  files instead of a database, and keywords extracted without an LLM.

※ recap: We're redesigning growi-search's research pipeline to be faster, more accurate and adaptive to any question, including mechanism questions that need several rounds of subagent research. Next step: your go-ahead to start building the section index on top of the mirror. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ remind of again of the other questions i asked before, a long list of q i     
  asked yo?                                                                     

● That long list came in the message before your last one. Here are the
  questions, each with the answer where it now stands:

  1. "The 3 paths aren't a spectrum. Make it adaptive to any query."
     Now two measured dials instead of fixed paths. WHERE (how many places hold
     relevant material) sets how many researchers run in each round. HOW FAR (is
     the answer written down, or must it be pieced together) sets how many
     rounds run.
  2. "How would you keep it in sync with GROWI live, with so many pages?"
     The existing mirror already polls GROWI every 10 s and keeps every page
     body on disk. A new background indexer compares page revisions against the
     mirror and re-processes only the pages that changed. Sections are hashed,
     so a moved or renamed page reuses its embeddings. Deleted pages have their
     sections dropped.
  3. "Where will you keep the embeddings?"
     In sections/vectors.npy, a float16 file inside the mirror folder, alongside
     hashes.json. It can't be a database because growi-search's tests forbid
     sqlite. Roughly 77 MB per 50k sections, searched with numpy.
  4. "Where will you store the keywords?"
     In memory, rebuilt from the mirror when the service starts. Only the
     embeddings are expensive enough to be worth saving to disk.
  5. "How do you get keywords per section quickly, without a full LLM pass?"
     With regex: exact names (mpf_mfs_open), katakana, kanji and latin word
     runs, headings, code, bold text and table headers, plus the known entity
     names from the 目次 cards. These are then ranked by TF-IDF, which takes
     milliseconds per section. Search still reads the full text, so a missed
     keyword never hides a section.
  6. "I still want the researcher/subagent + compiler pattern."
     It's kept. Researchers now get threads grouped by connection instead of by
     document. They're no longer blocked from reading each other's seeds, and
     they get a definition(name) tool.
  7. "Large questions may need a 2-stage compiler."
     It's kept. The switch is now by size: 1 stage if all reports fit in one LLM
     input, otherwise 2.
  8. "Fully adaptive: what if a query looks like a quick lookup but needs 
     subagents? For example 'what is file deadlock', where the answer is two 
     kinds of functions running together."
     The quick exit only happens when one section directly answers the question
     and the question isn't asking how or why. Otherwise researchers run, and
     after each round the lead checks for gaps, which triggers further rounds.
     The file deadlock case goes through 2 rounds.

  Two questions from earlier are still open:

  - Where the index comes from: the mirror, which is my pick, or the linker
    database. Your storage questions suggest the mirror, but you haven't
    confirmed it.
  - Should I start building? The first step would be the section indexer, then
    the search loop, then testing the fact path on mpf_mfs_open.

● I went through all four points. Here's where each stands.

  1. The sync freeze: three defects in mirror.py

  I couldn't reproduce the freeze here: the local GROWI has only 126 pages and
  every change API answers in about 0.03 s. What follows comes from reading the
  code. Any one of these three would freeze the mirror on the copy it cached
  first:

  defect A  sync_once (mirror.py:104)
    relist runs FIRST → the big /pages/list walk times out → exception
    → last_relist never updates → the relist is retried every 10 s, forever
    → change polling is never reached                            → frozen

  defect B  _poll_recent (mirror.py:449)
    the watermark is saved only after a FULL successful pass
    /pages/recent times out at work (the handoff says so)
    → the watermark never moves → every poll re-reads the same backlog and times
  out again

  defect C  _load (mirror.py:136)
    on restart, ready=True is set from the old catalog on disk
    → old pages are served silently; the only sign is "mirror sync deferred" in 
  the log
    → IndexMap follows mirror.version (researcher.py:412), so the 目次 map 
  freezes too

  To tell which one hit you, run this on the work PC:

  cat data/growi-search-mirror/*/meta.json        # which mode it uses and how 
  old last_relist is
  grep "mirror sync deferred" <uvicorn log> | tail # which endpoint is timing 
  out

  The real fix is to stop growi-search from syncing the index with GROWI at all
  (see §4). The mirror would then only hold page bodies, and defects A–C still
  need fixing for that job.

  2. Vector store: Qdrant

  Chroma is out. Its sparse (BM25) indexing works only in Chroma Cloud; a local
  install fails with "Sparse vector indexing is not enabled in local". Qdrant
  has had sparse vectors with IDF weighting and fused hybrid queries in its
  open-source server since 1.10.

  These are the Qdrant features we'd actually use:

  dense vectors (body, summary)   → meaning search
  sparse vector + IDF modifier    → BM25 keyword search (we supply the Japanese 
  tokens)
  prefetch + RRF fusion           → both searches combined in ONE query
  payload filters + indexes       → project / document / level / page_id / 
  entity
  group_by document               → "how many documents have hits" = the WHERE 
  dial
  facets on entity names          → group researchers' evidence by shared names
  scroll by document              → every section of a doc, in order (for lists)
  aliases                         → rebuild into a new collection, then swap 
  instantly
  snapshots                       → one shareable file (work PC ↔ here)

  Run it as a server (Docker or the single binary) with its data folder on disk.
  The publisher writes to it, and growi-search reads from it. Qdrant's "local
  file" mode (path=) allows only one process at a time, so it can't serve the
  builder and growi-search together.

  3. Keywords live in Qdrant, next to the vectors

  Each section of each page becomes one point that holds both vectors and all
  its metadata:

  id:       uuid5(chunk_id)                      (the linker's stable section 
  id)
  vectors:  body, summary (dense) + terms (sparse BM25)
  payload:  project, document, page_id, revision, path, title, heading, ordinal,
            line_start/end, level (section|page|document|folder),
            keywords[], entities[{name, role}], claims[], summary, points[], 
  kind, text

  The level field also covers the 3-level 目次 hierarchy from the addendum.
  Page, document and folder summaries are points in the same collection, so a
  JEV pass can decide whether to go a level deeper using a filter.

  4. Keywords at build time, and index sync owned by the publisher

  Build time. The builder already makes one LLM call per section, chunk_meta. It
  runs on the text before links are added, which satisfies the handoff's
  ordering rule. The call is also cached by text hash, and the linker already
  embeds each section. So we extend that same call rather than adding a new
  pass:

  today  (graph/linker/prompts.py)          →  after
  ≤12 keywords                              →  more keywords (e.g. 30), stronger
  prompt
  claims / bridge_probe removed (:85)       →  claims back (point-wise facts)
  1–3 sentence summary                      →  + points[] (point-wise summary) +
  kind
                                                ("API reference, parameters, 
  examples")

  One cost to know about: bumping CHUNK_META_VERSION invalidates the cache, so
  every section gets described once more. That's one LLM call per section, run
  at the current concurrency.

  Sync: the publisher becomes the only writer, so growi-search never polls GROWI
  for the index:

  publish sweep: page goes to GROWI → page_id + revision
    → upsert that page's section points (reuse the vectors the linker already 
  made)
    → delete points for sections that no longer exist
  move            → update the payload path only (no re-embed)
  delete          → delete points by page_id
  GROWI hand-edit → pull_growi_once (every 300 s in watch)
                    → re-split the page → only changed sections: chunk_meta + 
  embed → upsert

  The code already has hooks for all of this: _publish_sweep, _remove_sources,
  move_sources and pull_growi_once. The last one currently pulls edits without
  re-indexing, so it needs one addition.

  Freshness:
  - Builder output is indexed the moment it's published.
  - Hand edits in GROWI show up within growi_interval, which is 5 minutes by
    default.

  Decisions for you

  1. Only builder-published pages get indexed. That matches the rule "JEV only
     on pages reachable from a project-root 目次". Do handwritten GROWI pages
     outside the builder also need to be searchable?
  2. Can the work PC run a Qdrant server? Docker or the single binary both work.
  3. Is it OK to re-describe every section once after the chunk_meta prompt is
     extended?

  Sources:
  - Chroma issue #6185: BM25 for local setups
  - Chroma: sparse vector support
  - Qdrant 1.10: Universal Query, built-in IDF
  - Hybrid Search in Qdrant

● I've updated docs/new-growi-search.md so the builder never touches Qdrant. It
  only writes to GROWI, as it does today. The doc now reads:

  1. Builder
     - Extends its existing per-section chunk_meta call.
     - Writes everything into a JSON data block at the bottom of each 目次: page
       ids, revisions, summaries, points, kind, keywords, entities and facts,
       down to section level.
     - A small shared module (graph/common/mokuji_data.py) does render() for the
       builder and parse() for growi-search, so both sides always read the same
       format.
  2. Local Qdrant
     - Owned and built by growi-search from the 目次 blocks and the page bodies,
       with the embeddings computed by growi-search itself.
     - It runs in local file mode, so there's no server to set up.
     - The data can be thrown away: delete the folder and it rebuilds.
  3. Sync through a hash tree
     - Each parent 目次 stores a hash of each child's data block.
     - Every ~30 s, growi-search reads only the root 目次 of each project and
       goes down only into the parts that changed.
     - That removes the audit log, /pages/recent and the 30-minute relist, which
       were the three things that froze the old mirror. The mirror is deleted.
     - Hand edits in GROWI still come through: the builder's watch loop pulls
       them and rebuilds the 目次.

  The finder, the two dials, the research loop and the keep/delete table are
  unchanged.

  There are 4 open decisions at the bottom of the doc. The new one is whether
  the data block is visible to readers as a collapsed 検索用データ section, or
  fully hidden. Answer those and I'll append the full implementer guide.

● Update(docs/new-growi-search.md)
  ⎿  Added 82 lines
      208  COMPILER  reports are small → 1 stage → answer
      209  ```
      210  
      211 +### Speed: everything overlaps                                
      212 +                                                              
      213 +The search is one live process, not a line of stages. Every pa
          +rt starts the moment its input                                
      214 +exists and never waits for the part before it to finish. A sma
          +ll controller watches everything                              
      215 +and decides when the answer is good enough.                   
      216 +                                                              
      217 +**Lanes running at the same time** (one question, left to righ
          +t is time)                                                    
      218 +                                                              
      219 +```                                                           
      220 +time ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
          +────────────────────►                                         
      221 +                                                              
      222 +FIND        [search+rerank][JEV b1][JEV b2][JEV b3][JEV b4] · 
          +dry · dry → stops                                             
      223 +                              │ hit    │ hit           │ hit  
      224 +THREADS                    thread 1  thread 2        thread 3 
          +     (formed as soon as a                                     
      225 +                              │        │               │      
          +      cluster shows up)                                       
      226 +RESEARCH                   [researcher 1────────]      [resear
          +cher 3──────]                                                 
      227 +                                    [researcher 2─────────]   
          + [gap researcher──]                                           
      228 +                                           │ report       │ re
          +port       │ report                                           
      229 +LEAD CHECK                                 [check]        [che
          +ck]─gap─►  [check] → complete                                 
      230 +                                                            │ 
      231 +                                                            └─
          +► new search on the gap words                                 
      232 +                                                              
          +  (FIND wakes up again)                                       
      233 +COMPILER                                          [L1 fold]   
          +           [final ── streams ──►]                             
      234 +                                                              
      235 +UI          progress, found sections, researcher notes and ans
          +wer tokens stream the whole time                              
      236 +```                                                           
      237 +                                                              
      238 +- **FIND never blocks research.** The first thread starts whil
          +e JEV is still scoring batch 2.                               
      239 +- **Researchers never wait for each other.** Each report goes 
          +to the lead check the moment it                               
      240 +  lands, so there are no "rounds" to wait for. A gap starts it
          +s own new researcher right away.                              
      241 +- **Researchers look things up in parallel.** Their tool calls
          + (read, search, definition) all run                           
      242 +  at once, and every read is cached for everyone.             
      243 +- **The compiler folds as it goes.** L1 folds start as soon as
          + a batch of reports exists, and                               
      244 +  the final answer streams to the user token by token.        
      245 +                                                              
      246 +**The quick answer doesn't wait either**                      
      247 +                                                              
      248 +```                                                           
      249 +JEV finds a strong A (and the question isn't asking how or why
          +)                                                             
      250 +  → start writing the answer immediately (it streams to the us
          +er)                                                           
      251 +  → FIND keeps checking 1–2 more batches in the background    
      252 +       nothing better found            → keep the answer, stop
          + FIND                                                         
      253 +       a second strong A in another doc → redo the answer with
          + both (rare)                                                  
      254 +```                                                           
      255 +                                                              
      256 +**When is it done?** (the controller re-checks after every eve
          +nt)                                                           
      257 +                                                              
      258 +```                                                           
      259 +FIND stops         after 2+ batches in a row find nothing new 
      260 +                   (it waits longer while nothing at all has b
          +een found yet)                                                
      261 +a thread closes    when its researcher has reported and the le
          +ad check has no open gap for it                               
      262 +the run is DONE    when FIND has stopped AND no thread is open
          + AND no gap is open                                           
      263 +                   → final compiler → answer streams → check a
          +gainst the sources                                            
      264 +                   → cancel everything still running, so its L
          +LM slots free up at once                                      
      265 +hard stops         the user presses stop · time budget · gap-r
          +ound cap (~3)                                                 
      266 +```                                                           
      267 +                                                              
      268 +**Speed without overloading the shared LLM server**           
      269 +                                                              
      270 +The LLM server's concurrency stays where it is (house rule). S
          +peed comes from never leaving a                               
      271 +slot idle and giving each free slot to the most useful job:   
      272 +                                                              
      273 +```                                                           
      274 +next free LLM slot goes to:   1. final answer / quick answer  
          +  (closest to the user)                                       
      275 +                              2. lead check                   
          +   (unblocks new gaps)                                        
      276 +                              3. L1 fold                      
          +   (unblocks the final answer)                                
      277 +                              4. researcher, most promising th
          +read first                                                    
      278 +```                                                           
      279 +                                                              
      280 +Everything that isn't an LLM call has its own separate limit a
          +nd runs alongside:                                            
      281 +                                                              
      282 +```                                                           
      283 +JEV                → its own server and pool                  
      284 +embedder, reranker → small pools of their own                 
      285 +local Qdrant       → in-process, milliseconds                 
      286 +GROWI page reads   → cached, fetched ahead as soon as a sectio
          +n is accepted                                                 
      287 +```                                                           
      288 +                                                              
      289 +**How it's built**: one asyncio event loop per question. Each 
          +resource gets its own limit                                   
      290 +(semaphore), LLM slots are handed out through the priority que
          +ue above, and a single stop signal                            
      291 +cancels every task at once (the stop button, done, or a time b
          +udget).                                                       
      292 +                                                              
      293  ## What we keep and what we delete (`growi-search/`)
      294  
      295  | file                                                        
                      | fate

● I've added a Speed: everything overlaps section to docs/new-growi-search.md,
  right after the file deadlock example. It covers five things:

  1. A lanes diagram over time.
     - FIND, the threads, the researchers, the lead check, the compiler and the
       UI all run side by side.
     - Each lane starts the moment its input exists, so the first researcher
       starts while JEV is still scoring.
     - Each report goes to the lead check as soon as it lands, so there are no
       fixed rounds to wait for; a gap starts its own researcher immediately.
     - L1 folds start early, and the answer streams token by token.
  2. Quick answers start immediately. Once a section clearly answers a
     non-how/why question, the answer starts streaming right away. FIND keeps
     checking 1–2 more batches in the background in case something better turns
     up.
  3. When it's done. A small controller re-checks after every event:
     - FIND stops after 2+ empty batches in a row.
     - A thread closes once its researcher has reported and it has no open gap.
     - The run is done when both hold and no gap is open. Everything still
       running is then cancelled, so LLM slots free up at once.
     - Hard stops: the stop button, a time budget, and a cap of about 3 gap
       rounds.
  4. Speed without overloading the shared LLM server. Its concurrency stays
     where it is, per your rule. Instead, each free slot goes to whatever is
     closest to the answer: the final/quick answer first, then the lead check,
     then L1 folds, then the most promising researcher. JEV, the embedder, the
     reranker, Qdrant and GROWI reads each have their own limits and run
     alongside.
  5. How it's built. One asyncio event loop per question, a limit per resource,
     a priority queue for LLM slots, and one stop signal that cancels
     everything.

  I also added a note under the step-by-step diagram saying the steps actually
  overlap, and changed build order step 3 to "built async from day one".

● I've updated the doc so each step runs its work all at once and waits for all
  of it before the next step starts. The expensive LLM researchers now start
  only after every JEV answer is in, so a direct hit can still cancel them
  before they cost anything.

  What changed in docs/new-growi-search.md:

  - Finder and step-by-step diagram:
    - JEV scores a wave of about 100 sections at once and waits for every
      answer.
    - If hits still show up near the bottom, or nothing was found at all, one
      more wave runs, again all at once.
    - Researchers per round and gap researchers also run all at once, and the
      lead check reads all the reports.
  - The Speed section is now "run wide, then decide":
    - One top-to-bottom diagram with the cheap steps (JEV waves, no LLM) and the
      expensive steps (researchers, lead check) marked.
    - The quick exit sits between them, so a direct hit means no researcher ever
      starts.
    - A short "why each step waits" table:
      - JEV before researchers
      - all reports before the lead check
      - the lead check before compiling
    - Within a step, everything is still parallel: JEV per wave, researchers up
      to the LLM slot limit, each researcher's tool calls, L1 folds. The UI
      streams the whole time.
    - The done rules and the per-resource limits are updated to match.
  - Build order step 3: "per-step gather" replaces the old LLM priority queue.

  The file changed on disk while I was editing, so your editor may have it open
  again. Reload before you save.

● No, it was only partly in. The doc had the basics (more keywords, facts,
  points, kind) but was missing several handoff items and never said what Qdrant
  does with each field. Both are in docs/new-growi-search.md now.

  Part 1, builder: added to the same chunk_meta call
  - New fields:
    - kind: 「…の API リファレンス、パラメータ、使用例」, not just the subject's
      name.
    - points: kept general, so they don't go stale after small edits.
    - search_terms: every name, parameter, error code and value, plus the words
      a reader would type. No cap. This is your "stronger keyword logic".
    - facts: claims switched back on.
    - bridge: switched back on.
  - Linker fields stay as they are. keywords, entities and behaviours don't
    change, so link quality doesn't move. All names are collected in
    search_terms instead of widening entities.
  - Three fixes I found in the code:
    - model_text cuts each section at 12,000 characters and drops big tables
      before chunk_meta sees it. That's why parameter tables never reach the
      metadata.
    - validate_meta silently drops any entity without a kind, and the prompt
      never asks for one.
    - validate_meta keeps only 12 keywords and 20 claims. The new fields get no
      cap.
  - Page entries are built from their sections. Today they use only the first
    section's summary.
  - The data block holds everything, uncapped, across all 3 目次 levels. The
    caps only apply to the visible list.

  Part 2, yes, it all goes into Qdrant. The doc now has a diagram mapping each
  field to its vector or payload and what uses it:

  ┌─────────────────────────┬─────────────────┬────────────────────────────┐
  │          Field          │ Where it goes   │        What uses it        │
  │                         │    in Qdrant    │                            │
  ├─────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ section body (fetched   │ meaning vector  │ normal search              │
  │ from GROWI)             │ + BM25          │                            │
  ├─────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ search_terms            │ BM25            │ exact names                │
  ├─────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ summary, kind, points   │ "gist" vector   │ "what is this section      │
  │                         │                 │ about"                     │
  ├─────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ bridge                  │ its own vector  │ how/why questions like     │
  │                         │                 │ file deadlock              │
  ├─────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ each fact               │ its own small   │ needle hunts               │
  │                         │ point           │                            │
  ├─────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ entities                │ payload filter  │ definition(name)           │
  ├─────────────────────────┼─────────────────┼────────────────────────────┤
  │ behaviours              │ payload         │ grouping researchers'      │
  │                         │                 │ threads                    │
  └─────────────────────────┴─────────────────┴────────────────────────────┘

  One point per fact, plus splitting long sections into windows, gives the small
  searchable units that your "line-by-line summary" idea asked for, without an
  extra LLM pass.

  One new risk, added as open decision 5. With every fact included, a large
  document's data block could reach roughly 0.5–1 MB, and I haven't checked
  whether GROWI copes with pages that size. The fallback is one hidden child
  page per document holding the block. role_judge and the reference_research
  change are left out on purpose, with the reasons in the doc.

          +]) for doc in sorted(node.documents, key=str)]

      1101 +    async def _compile(self, run: Run, reports: list[Report])
           + -> str:                                                     
      1102 +        """One stage when everything fits one input; else L1 
           +folds, all at once, until it does.                           
      1103 +        The final stage sees only fold outputs, never the raw
           + reports next to them."""                                    
      1104 +        st = run.settings

      1108 +        fold_budget = st.llm_context_tokens - st.report_fold_
           +tokens - est_tokens(FOLD_PROMPT + head)

           +t:

           +n(level), "chars": sum(len(f) for f in folded)})

           +(est_tokens(t) for t in level)

           +om what we have

      128 +## JEV                                                        
      129  
      186 -## How retrieval works                                        
      130 +JEV ([chaoliangUNSW/Jev-Style-0.8B-Decision-v3](https://huggin
          +gface.co/chaoliangUNSW/Jev-Style-0.8B-Decision-v3))           
      131 +is the finder's judge, not an answer generator. For every sect
          +ion of a wave it answers                                      
      132 +two yes/no questions over one shared state, and it reads the q
          +uestion itself once                                           
      133 +(how/why? full list?). Without JEV the finder hands the top 20
          + reranked sections to the                                     
      134 +researchers and never takes the quick exit.                   
      135  
      188 -1. `/api/search` (and the router's first hop) makes **exactly 
          -one** GROWI                                                   
      189 -   `/_api/search` call and returns snippets in Elasticsearch o
          -rder — never page                                             
      190 -   bodies. Reranking is optional.                             
      191 -2. The optional index map reads the root `/<root>/00-目次` tre
          -e and document                                                
      192 -   indexes. It ranks page cards by term overlap when embedding
          -s are disabled;                                               
      193 -   embeddings and reranking can be enabled separately. Index r
          -efreshes are                                                  
      194 -   process-local and do not consume per-run page budgets.     
      195 -3. `/api/ask` runs the router: `shallow` answers from ≤ `WIKI_
          -SHALLOW_PAGE_READS`                                           
      196 -   hydrated pages; section reranking is optional. `deep` hands
          - the lead agent                                               
      197 -   the candidates. Failed hydration falls back to deep.       
      198 -4. The lead agent (`search` / `explore` / `finish`) spawns bou
          -nded parallel                                                 
      199 -   subagents (`read` / `follow_link` **outgoing-only** / `sear
          -ch` / `finish`).                                              
      200 -   Every page body and ES call is charged to a shared per-run 
          -budget                                                        
      201 -   (`WIKI_MAX_PAGE_FETCHES_PER_RUN`, `WIKI_MAX_SEARCH_CALLS_PE
          -R_RUN`); duplicate                                            
      202 -   reads/searches are memoized and the process-local TTL page 
          -cache                                                         
      203 -   (`WIKI_PAGE_CACHE_*`) is shared across subagents. With the 
          -mirror enabled,                                               
      204 -   page bodies are also persisted in its disk cache.          
      205 -                                                              
      206 -## Jev relevance gate (optional)                              
      207 -                                                              
      208 -Jev ([chaoliangUNSW/Jev-Style-0.8B-Decision-v3](https://huggin
          -gface.co/chaoliangUNSW/Jev-Style-0.8B-Decision-v3))           
      209 -is a per-question **relevance gate**, not an answer generator.
          - When enabled,                                                
      210 -each `/api/ask` question runs either the default exhaustive sw
          -eep or the opt-in                                             
      211 -cascade pipeline (`WIKI_JEV_MODE=cascade`). Keep exhaustive mo
          -de as the default                                             
      212 -until WP-15 meets its quality targets on the real corpus. In e
          -xhaustive mode,                                               
      213 -each question scores page entries in document `00-目次` pages 
          -reachable from a                                              
      214 -project root `00-目次`. With `GROWI_ROOT_PATH=/`, each first-l
          -evel project with a                                           
      215 -valid root index is a separate entry point. Unlisted pages are
          - not scored. A                                                
      216 -"yes" card is fetched as a research seed without a second, ful
          -l-body Jev check.                                             
      217 -Before cards are scored, the question is scoped against reacha
          -ble document                                                  
      218 -indexes:                                                      
      219 -                                                              
      220 -1. **Find document 目次** — from each project that has its own
          - root `00-目次`,                                              
      221 -   walk that project's page tree to every depth and read every
          - document-level                                               
      222 -   `00-目次`. The GROWI-level `/` is never treated as a projec
          -t root.                                                       
      223 -2. **Analyze intent and rank them** — an LLM first expands the
          - user's target,                                               
      224 -   requested range, required evidence, and exclusions. Jev the
          -n asks every 目次                                             
      225 -   whether it has concrete evidence for that scoped intent, on
          -e page                                                        
      226 -   at a time in `WIKI_JEV_TOC_CHUNK_TOKENS` chunks: Jev refuse
          -s an over-budget input                                        
      227 -   and never truncates, so one oversized 目次 must not sit in 
          -the same batch as the                                         
      228 -   pages scored beside it. The top 10% are kept, plus everythi
          -ng tied with them up to                                       
      229 -   the material budget (5 × 12,000 chars), because a wiki whos
          -e scores are all `0.00`                                       
      230 -   has no 10% to speak of.                                    
      231 -3. **Summarise the winners** — `TOC_NOTE_GROUPS` LLM calls (5)
          -, balanced by page size                                       
      232 -   so a huge 目次 gets its own call and small ones share, each
          - checking whether the                                         
      233 -   document has the requested evidence rather than merely shar
          -ing the topic.                                                
      234 -4. **Rewrite** — one call combines the original question, the 
          -initial intent, and                                           
      235 -   those notes into a page-target question whose breadth follo
          -ws the user's actual                                          
      236 -   range. Each document-index card is judged only against that
          - scoped requery, so                                           
      237 -   topic-only pages are rejected.                             
      238 -                                                              
      239 -Each stage is printed as it lands (`Jev 目次 digest: 24 文書 …
          -`, then `Jev query                                            
      240 -(rewritten from 24 目次 entries): …`), and a stage that fails 
          -says why instead of                                           
      241 -quietly sweeping with the raw question.                       
      242 -The gate asks whether each document-index entry indicates a pa
          -ge useful for the                                             
      243 -question. One thread scores cards while `WIKI_JEV_WORKERS` thr
          -eads fetch accepted                                           
      244 -pages for the lead and subagents. Page bodies are not sent to 
          -Jev in this sweep.                                            
      245 -                                                              
      246 -Confirmed seeds then drive the research directly: they are gro
          -uped by document                                              
      247 -and sliced into at most `WIKI_JEV_SUBAGENT_GROUPS` groups of a
          -t most                                                        
      248 -`WIKI_JEV_SUBAGENT_GROUP_SIZE` seeds, and each group gets its 
          -own subagent                                                  
      249 -(running `WIKI_SUBAGENT_CONCURRENCY` at a time). A group only 
          -ever sees its own                                             
      250 -seeds; reading a page another group owns is refused by the `re
          -ad` tool, so the                                              
      251 -groups follow genuinely new paths instead of overlapping. Repo
          -rts are folded in                                             
      252 -windows of 4 as they land. With eight or more explorer groups,
          - each completed                                               
      253 -window is submitted immediately to an independent level-1 comp
          -iler pool; the                                                
      254 -level-2 merge waits only for those completed folds. With fewer
          - than eight groups,                                           
      255 -raw reports go directly to the level-2/final compiler. Inputs 
          -are partitioned only                                          
      256 -when they exceed the configured model context; every slice is 
          -sent in full and no                                           
      257 -source text is discarded. Each fold streams `reports_folded` (
          -`reports`, `chars`).                                          
      258 -                                                              
      259 -A lead-agent call that still fails (proxy reset, model crash)

          - `lead_failed` and answers

      287 -| Variable | Meaning |                                        
      288 -| --- | --- |                                                 
      289 -| `WIKI_JEV_THRESHOLD` | candidate gate on card scores (defaul
          -t `0.50`). |                                                  
      290 -| `WIKI_JEV_SEED_THRESHOLD` | full-read seed gate (default `0.
          -80`, must be ≥ threshold). |                                  
      291 -| `WIKI_JEV_DEVICE` | local runtime device: `auto` (default), 
          -`cuda`, `mps`, `cpu`. |                                       
      292 -| `WIKI_JEV_DTYPE` | local runtime weights: `bfloat16` (defaul
          -t), `float16`, `float32`. |                                   
      293 -| `WIKI_JEV_MODEL_REVISION` | Optional Hugging Face commit pin
          - for the runtime and model snapshot. |                        
      294 -| `WIKI_JEV_MAX_BATCH_REQUESTS` | Engine request cap (default 
          -`64`). |                                                      
      295 -| `WIKI_JEV_LLM2JEV_CONCURRENCY` | concurrent single-state `/v
          -1/systemone` calls for the `llm2jev` backend (default `20`). |
      296 -| `WIKI_JEV_RECORD` / `WIKI_JEV_RECORD_MAX` | Optional JSONL p
          -ath and maximum requests to record for parity checks (default 
          -`300`). |                                                     
      297 -| `WIKI_JEV_CHUNK_TOKENS` / `WIKI_JEV_CHUNK_OVERLAP` | body ch
          -unking (defaults `25600` / `10000`). |                        
      298 -| `WIKI_JEV_TOC_CHUNK_TOKENS` | 目次 chunking for the rewrite 
          -stage's ranking (default `15000`), comfortably inside the mode
          -l's 25,600-token input ceiling so the question head also fits.
          - |                                                            
      299 -| `WIKI_JEV_WORKERS` | concurrent fetch+classify threads in th
          -e sweep pipeline (default `4`). The shared `jev/` engine owns 
          -model access on one worker while callers prepare requests. |  
      300 -| `WIKI_JEV_SUBAGENT_GROUP_SIZE` / `WIKI_JEV_SUBAGENT_GROUPS` 
          -| seeds per seed-group subagent / max such subagents per quest
          -ion (defaults `5` / `8`). Size `WIKI_JEV_SUBAGENT_GROUPS × WIK
          -I_JEV_SUBAGENT_GROUP_SIZE` pages into `WIKI_MAX_PAGE_FETCHES_P
          -ER_RUN` or the groups starve. |                               
      301 -| `WIKI_JEV_PREFILTER_MIN_OVERLAP` | keyword units a page must
          - share with the rewritten question before it gets an expensive
          - body score (default `2`, `0` = off). Dropped pages are counte
          -d in `jev_complete.prefiltered`: if that number climbs and see
          -ds collapse, even the rewrite missed the corpus's vocabulary —
          - lower this to `1`, then `0`. |                               
      302 -| `WIKI_JEV_MAX_PAGE_READS` / `WIKI_JEV_MAX_LIST_CALLS` | inde
          -pendent sweep safety valves; `0` = unlimited. The sweep is exh
          -austive and can cost many GROWI reads. |

          +CH_TOKENS`,                                                   
      151 +`WIKI_JEV_LLM2JEV_CONCURRENCY`, …) are read by the shared `jev
          +/` package.                                                   
      152  
      304 -The rewrite stage streams its own events before the sweep star
          -ts, so it is never a                                          
      305 -silent wait: `jev_toc_scan` (`documents` found, one activity l
          -ine), then one                                                
      306 -`jev_progress` with `stage: "toc"` per ranked page (`done` / `
          -total` / `percent` /                                          
      307 -`document` / `probability` / `yes`), `jev_toc_chunk` when a 目
          -次 chunk still cannot be                                      
      308 -scored, `jev_toc` per summary call, `jev_toc_digest` (`scanned
          -` / `kept` / `groups` /                                       
      309 -`chars`), `jev_toc_failed` when the stage gives up and the raw
          - question is used, and                                        
      310 -finally `jev_query`. The progress bar relabels itself `00-目次
          - スキャン` while                                              
      311 -`stage` is `toc`, so the two stages of one question are readab
          -le apart.                                                     
      312 -                                                              
      313 -Sweep reads use their own budget (never the per-run `WIKI_MAX_
          -PAGE_FETCHES_PER_RUN`)                                        
      314 -and warm the shared page cache. The sweep streams `jev_progres
          -s`                                                            
      315 -(`done` / `total` / `percent` plus `scored`, `yes`, `mean_yes_
          -probability` and                                              
      316 -`mean_yes_card_probability`; the total grows as the crawl disc
          -overs work, so the                                            
      317 -percent never recedes and ends at 100) plus the per-page `jev_
          -gate` /                                                       
      318 -`jev_complete` / `jev_unavailable` events. The frontend render
          -s `jev_progress` as                                           
      319 -one fixed 250px bar that fills in place with the running avera
          -ge of `p(はい)` next                                          
      320 -to it, and `jev_complete` prints a one-line                   
      321 -summary (`N seeds from M pages, top p=0.62`) so a sweep that c
          -lears no seed is                                              
      322 -visible instead of silently falling back to Elasticsearch. The
          - gate events                                                  
      323 -themselves stay invisible.                                    
      324 -The average is over **the verdicts leaning yes only** (`p > 0.
          -5`), because averaging                                        
      325 -over a corpus where most pages are irrelevant just accumulates
          - noes. Card and body                                          
      326 -verdicts are averaged separately — a card summary and a full p
          -age body are different                                        
      327 -distributions — and the yes *count* travels with the average, 
          -since an average over                                         
      328 -zero yeses and an average of zero confidence would otherwise l
          -ook identical. The                                            
      329 -console gets the same numbers as a heartbeat every 25 verdicts
      330 -(`Jev sweep: 63% 646/1019 scored=612 yes=18 avg_p=0.71 card_av
          -g_p=0.83`).                                                   
      331 -The `/score` wire contract (structured `state` + ordered `ques
          -tions` → ordered                                              
      332 -`p(はい)`) is **this service's adapter contract**, not a claim
          - that the Hugging                                             
      333 -Face page exposes a generic text-generation endpoint. All back
          -ends are selected                                             
      334 -inside the shared `jev/` package; growi-search only calls its 
          -`score_many` shim.                                            
      335 -Local mode checks a complete                                  
      336 -`WIKI_JEV_LOCAL_PATH` first; when it is unset, it checks the s
          -tandard Hugging                                               
      337 -Face cache. Only a missing/incomplete snapshot is downloaded, 
          -synchronously at                                              
      338 -server startup, before `/api/ready` can succeed. The local run
          -time dependencies                                             
      339 -are declared by this project, and a failed download/load fails
          - startup instead                                              
      340 -of deferring work to the first user query.                    
      341 -Local mode uses the reference PyTorch kernels unless `causal_c
          -onv1d` and                                                    
      342 -`flash-linear-attention` are installed; the sweep is correct w
          -ithout them but                                               
      343 -several times slower per page.
