● I've implemented the plan. Nothing has actually run: I only did static checks
  (compile, ruff on the changed files, and importing every changed module).
  There were no tests and no live calls, as you asked.

  Wiki builder, kept small (4 edited files, about 136 lines, plus 1 new file)
  - chunk_meta now also asks for kind, points and search_terms (no cap),
    switches claims and bridge back on, and asks for each entity's kind. The
    linker's own fields are untouched, so linking behaves as before.
  - meta_text() gives chunk_meta the whole section: no 12,000-character cut, big
    tables kept.
  - graph/common/mokuji_data.py (new) is the one shared format and parser for
    the data block, plus the section splitter growi-search uses.
  - publisher/index.py appends the data block to every document, folder and root
    目次. Each block holds the hash of every child's block, so any change moves
    every hash up to the root. The visible list also shows 情報の種類 and 要点
    per page.

  growi-search backend, rewritten
  - New files: store.py (local Qdrant) and sync.py (polls the root 目次 and
    walks down only where hashes changed).
  - Hand edits: a 5-minute revision sweep re-indexes pages that were edited in
    GROWI, even without a builder run.
  - researcher.py: JEV waves all at once → quick exit or researchers all at once
    → lead check over all reports → gap rounds → 1- or 2-stage compiler. The
    stop button cancels everything.
  - Also rewritten: config.py, gateway.py, prompts.py and the README. app.py
    keeps its routes and SSE events.
  - Deleted: the mirror, walker, cascade and eval scripts, plus the tests for
    them.

  Before it can work on the work PC:
  1. Install qdrant-client. It's in pyproject.toml, but uv.lock wasn't updated.
  2. Run the linker once. The metadata version changed, so it re-describes every
     section, which costs about as much as a first build.
  3. Republish the 目次 pages (main.py index).
  4. Remove any blank WIKI_EMBED_BASE_URL= / WIKI_RERANK_BASE_URL= lines from
     growi-search/.env. growi-search now reads these from the shared .env.

  Four choices I made where the plan was open; any can be changed:
  - Handwritten pages are not searched.
  - Folder summaries are built without an LLM.
  - The data block shows as a collapsed 「検索用データ」 section.
  - If an embedder is configured but unreachable, the service refuses to start.
    Otherwise it would wipe and rebuild the index twice.

  The biggest untested risk is size: a large document's data block could reach
  about 1 MB, and I don't know yet whether GROWI copes with that.

  docs/new-growi-search.md now has the handoff for the future agent on your work
  PC:
  - where the code differs from the plan
  - the rollout steps
  - a test plan covering every new module
  - the four frontend check questions with what each should show
  - known gaps

● The tuning guide is in the handoff and the JEV backend swap is done. Nothing
  was run: the checks were ruff, imports, and building each JEV backend from its
  alias.

  Tuning guide (end of docs/new-growi-search.md)
  - How to see what a question did: a field-by-field map of the trace file, the
    summary log line, and the chat activity lines.
  - A top-down chart to find which step failed: index → retrieval → JEV → quick
    exit → researcher → lead check → compiler.
  - Symptom tables (what you see → why → which knob or code line to change) for:
    - index problems and retrieval misses
    - JEV misjudging, including a step-by-step way to calibrate the two
      thresholds
    - exiting too early, and not exiting early enough
    - researcher, lead-check and compiler failures
    - too slow, too shallow, wrong information, follow-ups, errors
  - A human-feedback loop: feedback labels mapped to sections, a regression
    question set, a compare-after-every-change routine, and a tuning order.
  - A reference of every environment knob and every hard-coded constant, noting
    which changes need the index rebuilt.

  The guide needed some small code changes:
  - Logging: growi-search had no logging setup, so all its INFO lines (sync
    progress, "index ready", JEV stats) were silently dropped. They now show.
  - Trace: a per-question JSON trace, WIKI_SEARCH_TRACE_DIR, records every JEV
    score, each decision, every researcher's findings, the lead checks, the
    folds and the answer.
  - Researcher limits: planning this showed a researcher's seed sections plus a
    few long page reads could overflow a 131k context. I lowered the seed share
    to 0.25 (SEED_SHARE) and page reads to 20,000 characters (READ_CHARS).

  JEV backends swapped
  - hosted.py is now the vanilla POST /v1/systemone client (one state per call).
    The aliases systemone, sglang, vllm and jpt now point to it.
  - llm2jev.py now holds the custom batched POST /score logic, which used to be
    in hosted.py. Different states now go out in parallel.
  - The pool size setting is now WIKI_JEV_HTTP_CONCURRENCY; the old
    WIKI_JEV_LLM2JEV_CONCURRENCY still works.
  - Tests and README: the two backend test classes are swapped to match (not
    run), and the README is updated.

  Two things to check on the work PC:
  1. Rename your setting: anything set to llm2jev to reach a stock llm2jev
     server must now say hosted.
  2. Confirm the custom logic: the batched /score contract was the only batched
     HTTP code I could find anywhere (repo, history, stash,
     ~/Downloads/growi-llm-wiki, Trash). If your custom logic sent several
     states in one call, for example a {"requests": [...]} body, that code
     wasn't found. Point me to it or drop it into jev/backends/llm2jev.py.
